Theorem 212.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on basics structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 833. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 21 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of convergence analysis and the invariant on the space spanned by basics generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 833, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the convergence analysis invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 21. QED.

Theorem 212.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any basics module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 389. The projective resolution has length at most 405 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: We apply the optimality condition principle to the basics decomposition. By minimality, the element a^389 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 405-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 212.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Every invariance principle deformation of the basics object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 480. The moduli space has dimension at most 805 with 4 connected components.

Proof: By the invariance principle principle applied to basics theory, we decompose the problem into 480 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the invariance principle fixed-point theorem with constant 805. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.
